What began as a stressful few months of juggling overtime jobs, late‑night paperwork, and travel for bids gradually turned into a year of chronic financial pressure, postponed bills, and worries about possibly having to sell her tools or downsize her home. She started skipping makerspace meetings and improvisational theatre sessions to chase more work, but the extra jobs didn’t materialize, and she found herself sitting in her truck after shifts, unable to muster the drive to work on new proposals or paintings. When a close friend from the hiking club noticed she’d withdrawn from weekend hikes and seemed to be letting maintenance jobs slip through the cracks, they urged her to speak with a primary care provider about how overwhelmed and stuck she felt, leading to a referral for psychiatric evaluation.\n- Chief Complaint: “I don’t care about the projects and hobbies I used to love anymore; nothing feels interesting to me now.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Pretty much every day I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest, like something terrible has happened even when nothing new has, and it just hangs over me from the first cup of coffee until I crawl back into bed.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to get excited about tinkering with solar setups, painting, or going to improv and the hiking club, but now even on days off I just sit in my truck or on the couch staring at my tools or the TV, feeling no spark at all and not caring if I ever do any of it again.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): Most days I have to force myself to eat because everything tastes like cardboard, and I’ll realize it’s late afternoon and I’ve only had a few crackers or a granola bar, which is the opposite of how I used to be around food.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Lately I lie in bed for hours with my mind running through bills and missed jobs, then when I finally do fall asleep I wake up at three or four in the morning and just stare at the ceiling, exhausted but unable to drift off again.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Out on jobs I move and talk like I’m wading through mud—my coworkers joke that I’m in slow motion—but inside I feel this restless, buzzing tension, like I want to crawl out of my own skin and yet can’t get myself to actually do anything.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep telling myself I’m a screwup for letting the business stall, that I’ve wasted all these years and don’t deserve to own a home or have nice things when I can’t even keep up with my bills or return calls on time.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): It’s gotten to where I’ll read the same email or equipment spec sheet three or four times and still can’t hold the details in my head, and even simple choices like what to cook or which invoice to tackle first can leave me frozen for half an hour.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the really bad nights, when I’m staring at the stack of overdue notices, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up tomorrow, and I’ve pictured crashing my truck on the highway, even though I haven’t made any plans to actually do it.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve started missing bid deadlines, turning in repair reports late, and canceling on friends so often that my income has dropped and I mostly just go straight home after work to sit alone, letting chores and bills pile up around me.\n- duration: This has been going on for a little over a year now, ever since I lost that big solar contract and the money started getting really tight.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I don’t care about the projects and hobbies I used to love anymore; nothing feels interesting to me now. I used to be so active with different things, but lately, I just can't find the spark to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me when you first started feeling this way and if it's been constant since then or if your feelings about these activities have changed over time?\n\nPatient: It started a little over a year ago when I lost a major solar contract and the financial stress got really bad, and it's been constant ever since. My passion for things like painting, improv, and hiking has completely vanished. Now, I mostly just sit on the couch or in my truck, feeling absolutely no spark to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns since you lost interest in your hobbies and activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, both have been really off. I have to force myself to eat because everything tastes like cardboard, and I'll often realize it's late afternoon and I've only had a few crackers. At night, I lie awake for hours worrying about bills, and then I wake up at three or four in the morning exhausted but unable to fall back asleep.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your feelings of sadness or hopelessness have changed over the past year, and have you had any periods where you felt significantly better or back to normal for a couple of months before worsening again?\n\nPatient: Pretty much every day for a little over a year, I've had this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest that just hangs over me from morning till night. I haven't had any periods where I felt back to normal or even slightly better; it has just been a continuous, chronic struggle since I lost that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of significant improvement in your mood or interest in activities that lasted for at least two months since the onset of your symptoms, or has it been a persistent state?\n\nPatient: No, it has been a completely persistent state without any breaks or improvement. Ever since I lost that solar contract about a year ago, this heavy mood and lack of interest have been there every single day. There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?"
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.

There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.

Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life."

My passion for things like painting, improv, and hiking has completely vanished. Now, I mostly just sit on the couch or in my truck, feeling absolutely no spark to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns since you lost interest in your hobbies and activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, both have been really off. I have to force myself to eat because everything tastes like cardboard, and I'll often realize it's late afternoon and I've only had a few crackers. At night, I lie awake for hours worrying about bills, and then I wake up at three or four in the morning exhausted but unable to fall back asleep.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your feelings of sadness or hopelessness have changed over the past year, and have you had any periods where you felt significantly better or back to normal for a couple of months before worsening again?\n\nPatient: Pretty much every day for a little over a year, I've had this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest that just hangs over me from morning till night. I haven't had any periods where I felt back to normal or even slightly better; it has just been a continuous, chronic struggle since I lost that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of significant improvement in your mood or interest in activities that lasted for at least two months since the onset of your symptoms, or has it been a persistent state?\n\nPatient: No, it has been a completely persistent state without any breaks or improvement. Ever since I lost that solar contract about a year ago, this heavy mood and lack of interest have been there every single day. There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first episode of depression, but can you confirm if you've had any periods of elevated mood or excessive energy that lasted for a few days to a week, where you felt unusually happy, irritable, or had less need for sleep, either before or during this current episode?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've ever had periods like that at all. My mood has mostly just been consistently low, heavy, and exhausted, rather than unusually high or energetic."

There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first episode of depression, but can you confirm if you've had any periods of elevated mood or excessive energy that lasted for a few days to a week, where you felt unusually happy, irritable, or had less need for sleep, either before or during this current episode?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've ever had periods like that at all. My mood has mostly just been consistently low, heavy, and exhausted, rather than unusually high or energetic.\n\nDoctor: Given that you've experienced this persistent low mood and loss of interest for over a year without any significant improvement, can you confirm whether you believe this is a single, continuous episode of depression or if you think there might have been any subtle variations that could suggest more than one episode?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like one single, continuous struggle without any breaks. Ever since I lost the big solar contract and the financial pressure set in, it has just been one long, unbroken stretch of feeling like this. There haven't been any periods where things got better or felt different."
  }

There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first episode of depression, but can you confirm if you've had any periods of elevated mood or excessive energy that lasted for a few days to a week, where you felt unusually happy, irritable, or had less need for sleep, either before or during this current episode?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've ever had periods like that at all. My mood has mostly just been consistently low, heavy, and exhausted, rather than unusually high or energetic.\n\nDoctor: Given that you've experienced this persistent low mood and loss of interest for over a year without any significant improvement, can you confirm whether you believe this is a single, continuous episode of depression or if you think there might have been any subtle variations that could suggest more than one episode?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like one single, continuous struggle without any breaks. Ever since I lost the big solar contract and the financial pressure set in, it has just been one long, unbroken stretch of feeling like this. There haven't been any periods where things got better or felt different.\n\nDoctor: You've confirmed this is your first episode of depression, lasting over a year without significant improvement. Can you think of any factors or events that might have contributed to the persistence of your symptoms, rather than a more episodic course?\n\nPatient: The financial stress just hasn't let up at all, which is why I think it's been so constant. Since losing that contract, I've been drowning in outstanding equipment loans and irregular income, all while constantly worrying that I'll have to sell my tools or downsize my home. The pressure is there every single day, so I never get a break from it."

I have to force myself to eat because everything tastes like cardboard, and I'll often realize it's late afternoon and I've only had a few crackers. At night, I lie awake for hours worrying about bills, and then I wake up at three or four in the morning exhausted but unable to fall back asleep.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how your feelings of sadness or hopelessness have changed over the past year, and have you had any periods where you felt significantly better or back to normal for a couple of months before worsening again?\n\nPatient: Pretty much every day for a little over a year, I've had this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest that just hangs over me from morning till night. I haven't had any periods where I felt back to normal or even slightly better; it has just been a continuous, chronic struggle since I lost that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of significant improvement in your mood or interest in activities that lasted for at least two months since the onset of your symptoms, or has it been a persistent state?\n\nPatient: No, it has been a completely persistent state without any breaks or improvement. Ever since I lost that solar contract about a year ago, this heavy mood and lack of interest have been there every single day. There hasn't been any time, let alone two months, where things felt back to normal.\n\nDoctor: You've described a persistent state of low mood and loss of interest for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if there have been any periods, even brief, where your symptoms seemed to worsen or if they've remained fairly constant in intensity?\n\nPatient: While it has mostly felt like a constant, heavy fog over the past year, it definitely gets much worse on certain nights. It feels especially overwhelming when I'm sitting alone, staring at the stack of overdue bills and notices. Those are the times when the weight of everything really crushes me.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe if there are any specific times of the year, seasons, or events that trigger a noticeable change in your mood or interest levels, even if it's just a slight variation from your usual state?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any seasonal patterns or times of the year that make a difference in how I feel. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first episode of depression, but can you confirm if you've had any periods of elevated mood or excessive energy that lasted for a few days to a week, where you felt unusually happy, irritable, or had less need for sleep, either before or during this current episode?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've ever had periods like that at all. My mood has mostly just been consistently low, heavy, and exhausted, rather than unusually high or energetic.\n\nDoctor: Given that you've experienced this persistent low mood and loss of interest for over a year without any significant improvement, can you confirm whether you believe this is a single, continuous episode of depression or if you think there might have been any subtle variations that could suggest more than one episode?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like one single, continuous struggle without any breaks. Ever since I lost the big solar contract and the financial pressure set in, it has just been one long, unbroken stretch of feeling like this. There haven't been any periods where things got better or felt different.\n\nDoctor: You've confirmed this is your first episode of depression, lasting over a year without significant improvement. Can you think of any factors or events that might have contributed to the persistence of your symptoms, rather than a more episodic course?\n\nPatient: The financial stress just hasn't let up at all, which is why I think it's been so constant. Since losing that contract, I've been drowning in outstanding equipment loans and irregular income, all while constantly worrying that I'll have to sell my tools or downsize my home. The pressure is there every single day, so I never get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: You've described a single, continuous episode of depression lasting over a year. Can you confirm if you've experienced any brief periods, even just a few weeks, where you felt nearly back to your normal self before the depressive symptoms returned?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had even a brief period of feeling like myself again. It has been a continuous, heavy struggle every single day for the past year since my business stalled, with absolutely no relief. It just feels like one long, unbroken weight."
  }
]

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I don’t care about the projects and hobbies I used to love anymore; nothing feels interesting to me now. I used to be so active with different things, but lately, I just can't find the spark to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me when you first started feeling this way and if it's been constant since then or if your feelings about these activities have changed over time?\n\nPatient: It started a little over a year ago when I lost a major solar contract and the financial stress got really bad, and it's been constant ever since. My passion for things like painting, improv, and hiking has completely vanished. Now, I mostly just sit on the couch or in my truck, feeling absolutely no spark to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns since you lost interest in your hobbies and activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, both have been really off. I have to force myself to eat because everything tastes like cardboard, and I'll often realize it's late afternoon and I've only had a few crackers. I haven't had any periods where I felt back to normal or even slightly better; it has just been a continuous, chronic struggle since I lost that contract.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of significant improvement in your mood or interest in activities that lasted for at least two months since the onset of your symptoms, or has it been a persistent state?\n\nPatient: No, it has been a completely persistent state without any breaks or improvement. Ever since I lost that solar contract about a year ago, this heavy mood and lack of interest have been there every single day. The only real trigger for all of this was losing that major solar contract and the constant financial stress that followed. It has just been a steady weight since then, regardless of the season.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression in the past that lasted for less than two years, or is this the first time you've felt this way for such an extended period?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never really experienced anything like this before. This is definitely the first time I’ve felt this way for such a long period of time. Before this past year, I was always able to handle stress and keep moving forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes of depression that were shorter in duration, or is this the first episode you've experienced, and if so, how does it compare to your current state in terms of severity and impact on daily life?\n\nPatient: This is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this. I've never had any shorter episodes in the past, so I don't have anything to compare it to. It's completely new to me and has totally disrupted my life.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this is your first episode of depression, but can you confirm if you've had any periods of elevated mood or excessive energy that lasted for a few days to a week, where you felt unusually happy, irritable, or had less need for sleep, either before or during this current episode?\n\nPatient: I don't think I've ever had periods like that at all. My mood has mostly just been consistently low, heavy, and exhausted, rather than unusually high or energetic.\n\nDoctor: Given that you've experienced this persistent low mood and loss of interest for over a year without any significant improvement, can you confirm whether you believe this is a single, continuous episode of depression or if you think there might have been any subtle variations that could suggest more than one episode?\n\nPatient: It has definitely felt like one single, continuous struggle without any breaks. Ever since I lost the big solar contract and the financial pressure set in, it has just been one long, unbroken stretch of feeling like this. There haven't been any periods where things got better or felt different.\n\nDoctor: You've confirmed this is your first episode of depression, lasting over a year without significant improvement. Can you think of any factors or events that might have contributed to the persistence of your symptoms, rather than a more episodic course?\n\nPatient: The financial stress just hasn't let up at all, which is why I think it's been so constant. Since losing that contract, I've been drowning in outstanding equipment loans and irregular income, all while constantly worrying that I'll have to sell my tools or downsize my home. The pressure is there every single day, so I never get a break from it.\n\nDoctor: You've described a single, continuous episode of depression lasting over a year. Can you confirm if you've experienced any brief periods, even just a few weeks, where you felt nearly back to your normal self before the depressive symptoms returned?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had even a brief period of feeling like myself again. It has been a continuous, heavy struggle every single day for the past year since my business stalled, with absolutely no relief. It just feels like one long, unbroken weight.\n\nDoctor: You've had a continuous episode of depression for over a year without significant improvement, but can you tell me if you've had any brief periods of feeling slightly better, even if it was just for a few days, or has it been a completely unbroken period of depressive symptoms?\n\nPatient: No, it has been completely unbroken with no relief at all. Ever since I lost the solar contract, every single day has felt heavy and difficult. I haven't had even a few days where I felt slightly better."

Ever since I lost the big solar contract and the financial pressure set in, it has just been one long, unbroken stretch of feeling like this. There haven't been any periods where things got better or felt different.\n\nDoctor: You've confirmed this is your first episode of depression, lasting over a year without significant improvement. Can you think of any factors or events that might have contributed to the persistence of your symptoms, rather than a more episodic course?\n\nPatient: The financial stress just hasn't let up at all, which is why I think it's been so constant. Ever since I lost the solar contract, every single day has felt heavy and difficult. Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nThis is the absolute first time I've ever experienced an episode of depression like this.
